- **Main CI on the phase 5 merge (2026-10-06; cloud session; branch `claude/epic-knuth-8mexwv` from `main` `a5bc3ce` = PR #44 merged):** run 37473357337 red on one test, `onboarding.spec.ts:270` (desktop), in its cleanup: `removeFixturePerson`'s member delete answered 409 (`activity_log_actor_id_fkey`), so staging's deploy was skipped. **Cause (the e2e harness, not the app):** the person's page was still open, and its app-open report (5.4, a background write audited with them as the actor) landed between the cleanup's `activity_log` delete and its `members` delete. Reproduced deterministically (the report held in the browser, let through after the `activity_log` delete: the same 409). **Fix:** the e2e Supabase proxy can fence a person (`POST /__fence`, `fencePerson()`): it refuses their later requests and answers once none of theirs is still at the upstream; `removeFixturePerson` fences before any delete. Proved by `tests/hold-proxy.test.ts` (the fence waits for the write in flight, then refuses theirs only; fails when the fence answers at once) and `e2e/fixture-cleanup.spec.ts` (a held report released mid-removal writes nothing and the removal passes; fails without the fence). No retry, no timeout. **Also (PR #45's own CI, 20:47 IST):** pgTAP 61's fixture tasks were due at 20:30 IST *today*, which `task_create` refuses once passed, so the file failed in any run after 20:30 IST (main's too); its section 1 now runs on tomorrow's IST day. Reproduced locally at 20:53 IST, then green. **Then a sweep of every pgTAP file for the same trap** (owner, 2026-10-06; fixed clock times "today" the app refuses once passed, `now() ± N` meant as today, jobs at real `now()` vs fixed-time fixtures): four more, each a window before midnight IST, fixed test-only: **24** (cutoff `23:59:59` → `24:00`, refused in the last second; the default `end_not_recorded()` run counted only for dates before today, since from 23:59 its job day is today), **28** (`cutoff_from_now(±1)` wrapped past midnight as a `time`, so "a minute before/after the cutoff" broke 23:59–00:01; now moved as an interval and held at 24:00 / 00:00; its `23:59:59` → `24:00`), **40** (from 23:59 the catch-up `absent_check()` covers today and its row for staff1 broke the next insert on the unique key; today's rows cleared first), **55** (a claim at `now() + 1 s` fell on tomorrow's cap day in the last second; now at `now()`, the digest a second older). Every other file read and judged safe at any hour. The VM clock could not be moved (refused: it is shared by the container), so each old and new expression was evaluated at 20:45, 23:50, 23:59:30, 23:59:59.5, 00:00:30 and 00:10 IST with explicit timestamps: every old one fails in its window, every new one holds. Production untouched; no tag.

- **The view's address (2026-10-02; Opus 5.5, cloud session; branch `fix/view-switch-reload` from `main` `3591d21` = v1.3.1 merged; not tagged: owner decision 2026-10-02, no production release until phase 5 is complete, this fix ships with the PR #41 fixes and 5B):** main's CI on the PR #41 merge (run 36943843968) caught a full reload of the task page on a Details / Activity switch (`task-page.spec:707`). **Root cause (Next.js 16.3.5–16.3.8, not our logic):** the view switch wrote `?tab=` with a native `history.replaceState`, which Next turns into a router restore keeping the route tree saved for the old query; a restore landing while a refresh of the screen is in flight (Realtime answering a notification, refresh on return, pull to refresh, an action that revalidates) leaves the refresh answering for a screen that moved on, and Next takes that for a tree mismatch and loads the address in full (`ppr-navigations.js` `dispatchRetryDueToTreeMismatch` → `completeHardNavigation`). Reproduced deterministically (a refresh held at the network, then a switch: a reload every time; no switch, or a switch before the refresh: none). **Fix:** `replaceViewAddress()` (`core/ui/navigation/view-address.ts`) is the one way a view control writes its address: the view changes on the tap, the address waits while the router has a fetch in flight (`NavProgress` counts screen fetches and actions' calls, `screen-fetches.ts`) and is written on that fetch's own completion (read to the end or failed), never after a timer; the last of two switches wins; a move elsewhere drops it. A revalidating action's answer also waits for Next's commit of it, read from Next's private `__NA` history marker (`history-writes.ts`; what breaks if Next renames it and which test turns red: ARCHITECTURE §14.2 d). Callers moved: the task page's views (`task-view.tsx`) and the list filters (`data-table.tsx`, Tasks and Clients). Lint forbids any `replaceState`/`pushState` given an address elsewhere (`eslint.config.mjs` `VIEW_ADDRESS_SELECTORS`, fixture `denied-view-address.ts`). **Tests:** `e2e/view-address.spec.ts` at 375 and 430px (a held refresh with switches on the task page and on the Tasks list's filter; a switch before a refresh; a switch while Task Noted's answer is held, one back leaves; red without the hold, without the commit wait and with the marker renamed), unit `view-address.test.ts`; `task-page.spec:308` now waits for the address before its reload. **Known, not covered (Ideas / tech debt, "The view's address, Next's remaining hazards"):** a back that closes a layer during a refresh after a switch still reloads. **For 5B (phase-5b's new tabs: Leave | Attendance, Extra work | Expenses):** a tab kept in the address without a navigation writes it only through `replaceViewAddress()`; a tab that is a route uses `ViewLink` as before.

Commits: `5d8d21f` (the unit), `429d838` (the hidden coordinator's name, archived field values on an edit), then docs. **Settings left for 4C (with the reason):** **Task types** (the Owner's editor, kickoff 4 decision 15): the dialog works with the seven seeded types; the editor needs `task_type_move()` (SQL, 4A later item (b)) and its default reminders are 5.3's editor; when built, its Settings row's permission becomes `settings.manage` (today `lists.manage` shows Admins a "Coming soon" row for a list they will never edit). **Per-type task fields** in Settings → Custom fields (the column and the dialog support them; the Settings form offers company-wide task fields only). **Decisions inside the kickoff's answers (a reviewer should confirm):** (a) one dialog creates and edits; the approver changes only through the ⋯ "Change approver" (the Owner), never in the edit form; stages are typed at creation and afterwards added or removed (unticked) on the page, no rename; (b) warnings on an edit: the people added, or everyone kept when the deadline's day or the event moved, the task's own count and block excluded; (c) at most one warning per kind per person, leave on the deadline's day first; (d) saving waits for a running availability check, and a failed check never blocks (it says so); (e) back with typed changes asks "Discard?" (§14.2 f), which the app's older form dialogs do not; (f) the client label pre-selects its Admin on create only; (g) the task page is one screen with sections, no tabs, history the latest 50 entries; (h) someone who may not see a task gets the not-found screen (RLS decides, as for clients); (i) a coordinator who is not an assignee acts (Start, ticks, comment default) for the freelancer they coordinate on the task, the primary one first; (j) clients offered as labels: draft, active and paused; (k) "Task Noted" is the page's one red commit, Mark done and Approve are neutral-solid triggers, Request changes a red outline; (l) a stage tick's time is the server's clock, `done_by` the guard's; (m) a Staff co-assignee who cannot see a freelancer's coordinator reads "Asha's coordinator" instead of the pair; (n) the Tasks stand-in's copy for the Owner and Admins now says New task is here and the list is coming. Details in "4B mechanics" below. **What 4C inherits:** the task page and its actions (a list row only needs `DrillLink href=/tasks/<id>`); `NewTaskButton` and `loadTaskFormSetup`; `taskActions()` and `statusLine()` for "Needs you" and "My tasks"; the `(list)` group (replace its page, `loading.tsx` and `STAND_INS.tasksTeam` / `tasksMine`, and `loading-routes.test.ts`'s stand-in lines); read names for Staff with `listDirectoryOf(ids)`, never the whole directory (mechanics (1)). **After the unit (the 4B fixer, Opus 5.5, 2026-09-29):** `ac1a869` fixes CI run 36602212936's only red (the Settings 200% test measured the rows while React still held the streamed page in its `<div hidden>`, so every label read 0px; it now waits for `expectSettled`), `2f18d4d` makes the independent review's fixes (migration `20260929172600_phase4b_review_fixes` with pgTAP `35`: S5 the guard stamps a tick's time, S6 no label on an Inactive client, S7 an archived type kept but never chosen; S1–S4, the S8 interim, S11 and S12 in the app, with the later items under Ideas "4B later items (review)" and the PR #22 merge list in "4B mechanics (8)") and `d6d9028` adds the review's e2e (M1's back specs, S10's flows), whose first full run found what `49e39ce` fixes (a modal reopened while it still closed put its new overlay over its own sheet, blurred and untappable; Dialog, AlertDialog and Sheet now key their portals by the opening).

Three expand-only migrations, no UI. **`20260929053632_freelancers`** (ADR-0013): `engagement` enum + `members.engagement` (default `permanent`), `members.email` nullable with `members_email_matches_engagement` (`(engagement = 'permanent') = (email is not null)`), the FK `members.id → auth.users` dropped (a freelancer has no auth row), `engagement` protected and refused on an API insert, `member_coordinators` (history, one current; RLS `team.view` + the coordinator's own rows; no API writes; audited `coordinator_set` / `coordinator_changed` / `coordinator_closed`), `app.coordinator_of()`, `member_add_freelancer()`, `member_set_coordinator()` (allowed on a deactivated freelancer, so a reactivation is prepared with it), `member_invite_employee()` ("Invite as employee": same id, `permanent` + email, `status invited`, `joined_at` cleared, the coordinator row closed `became_employee`), `member_invite()` / `member_deactivate()` (a coordinator with an active freelancer is `CONFLICT`; a freelancer's row is closed with them) / `member_reactivate()` (a freelancer needs a current coordinator: `INVALID_STATE`) / `member_change_email()` (a freelancer: `INVALID_STATE`) re-created with the refusals, `member_directory` + `engagement` and widened to the people on the caller's visible tasks and a coordinator's freelancers (`app.directory_visible()`), `activity_log.on_behalf_of_id` (the audit trigger reads the override's `on_behalf_of` key), the `permanent`-only guards (`app.attendance_require_self()`: every member-side attendance, leave, extra work and expense function; `comp_leave_grant()`; `app.absent_check()`; `app.end_day_reminder_due()`; `attendance_today_detail()`; `month_summary()`), `workload_warning_threshold` default 4 and set to 4. **`20260929053634_tasks_schema`**: the five enums, `task_types` (seeded per organization by `app.seed_org_task_types()` + backfill; RLS writes `settings.manage` and `app.task_types_owner_guard()`; column grant without `position` / `is_system`), `field_definitions.task_type_id` FK (+ the guard's NOT_FOUND and `has_values` for tasks), `tasks` (with `event_date` beside the optional times, `submitted_by` / `submitted_on_behalf_of`, `search` tsvector; **no API writes at all**), `task_assignees`, `task_stages` (API: a manager's edits, a worker's ticks, on behalf; `app.task_stages_guard()`), `task_comments` (API insert only; `app.task_comments_guard()`), `task_reviews`, `task_submissions`, `task_warnings` (+ `member_id`), every child table audited with `entity_id = task_id`, `app.task_visible()` as the one RLS gate (Owner all; created / approving / assigned / labelled with an assigned client / a coordinator's current freelancers), `app.is_task_assignee()`, `app.is_approving_admin()`, `app.task_manager()`, `app.task_on_behalf_ok()`, `app.labelled_client_ids()` filled in (Staff see the labels on their tasks), `app.task_custom_fields_guard()` (validates inside the functions too), `member_availability(from_date, to_date, member_ids)` (counts, event blocks, leave incl. `requested`, presence today; freelancers count, never with leave). **`20260929053636_task_transitions`**: `task_create` (route resolution, the type's field rules, stages with `app.next_position()`, reminder defaults, overridden warnings), `task_acknowledge`, `task_start`, `task_submit_done` (implied acknowledgement, versions, late reason, Admin-step skip), `task_review` (the Owner never at the Admin step; bulk = per-id calls), `task_reopen`, `task_cancel`, `task_update_assignment(task_id, changes jsonb, warnings)` (returns the changed fields; the trigger's diff is the field-level audit), `task_set_approver`; the on-behalf paths through `app.task_actor()` (a freelancer's own id is never an actor). **Proof:** `pnpm check` green (1013 unit in 82 files, pgTAP 2387 in 34 files: `31_freelancers` 121, `32_tasks_schema` 113, `33_task_transitions` 198, `00` lists the 8 new internal helpers, every file's teardown deletes the task and coordinator rows, `02` the seeded task types; build; budget `/approvals` 847.1 KB, `/my-day` and `/today` 773.6 KB, `/login` 526.0 KB); full e2e after one `db:reset` at 3 workers **603 passed / 8 failed / 109 skipped / 56 did not run, 16.0 min (started 09:32 UTC). The 8 red are the machine, not the diff: `expenses.spec.ts:69` and `extra-work.spec.ts:58` (the two ~200-step serial tests) on all three projects reach their last steps at 30 s and hit the test timeout with every step uniformly slow (load average 15–19 on 4 cores; the cloud container restarted at 09:15 UTC runs the suite in 16 min against 10.8 in the 3cB session), and `/reports/month` at 200% text on the phone projects overflows on the approved-unpaid claim the interrupted expenses spec leaves behind (a 3b.4 layout item, Ideas (f)); a **control run of the same two specs alone on the pre-4A schema** (the three migrations held aside, `db:reset`) failed the same way (6 failed at 32–34 s), and two further full runs gave 602/9 (one `/today` held-skeleton 5 s expect) and 603/8. No timeout raised; CI decides**; the architecture review (the `architecture-reviewer` checklist run in-session: this session had no subagent tool) found no must-fix; its four should-fixes are in the review-fix commit (`task_reopen` / `task_cancel` check `tasks.create` as well as the manager scope; `member_directory` shows a phone only to `team.view`, the person and a freelancer's current coordinator, never to a co-worker seen through a shared task; `task_update_assignment` answers `VALIDATION` to a malformed value instead of a raw cast error; `app.coordinator_eligible()` locks the coordinator's row so a deactivation racing an add or a change waits and then counts); later items under Ideas. **Decisions inside the kickoff's answers (a reviewer should confirm):** (a) a deactivated freelancer's coordinator row is closed, and reactivation needs `member_set_coordinator()` first (two calls, the handover pattern: no signature change to `member_reactivate`); (b) `task_templates` and `task_requests` are 4.6's tables (their `created_by` note is recorded in DATA-MODEL §6), so `tasks.template_id` has no FK yet; (c) the Owner cannot decide at the Admin step (`INVALID_STATE`), the Owner's way is `task_set_approver`; removing the approver of a `submitted` task, or naming an assignee, sends it to the Owner at once (`none` / `skipped`); (d) `task_start` implies no acknowledgement; (e) event tasks carry `event_date` (required) beside optional `event_start_at` / `event_end_at`, `purpose` only on an event, `location` only when the type `has_location` (Posting has none); (f) comments are allowed in any state to anyone who sees the task; (g) bulk approve is the app calling `task_review` per id (as the attendance bulk), no SQL bulk function; (h) `member_change_email()` refuses a freelancer. Details in "4A mechanics" below. - **6A unblocked (2026-10-07): `notifications.spec:754` [mobile-lg] explained and fixed in the app (issue #49, the advisor's option A).** Diagnosis on the throwaway branch `diag/6a-bell-reload` (that one test, 30 repeats, at mobile-lg, with the tap's state logged by the e2e helpers; deleted): run 37612077103 **1 red of 30**, run 37612986171 0 of 30 with the probe repaired, run 37614306577 0 of 30 with the fix. What the logs showed: every `?_rsc` before the surprise document load is a prefetch (`Next-Router-Prefetch: 1`: the four tab links' segment-cache `/_tree` prefetches, aborted and re-issued as legacy full-tree prefetches, and a `metadata-only` prefetch of `/my-day` itself), so no refresh path was involved; in a green repeat the bell's own `/notifications` prefetch appears at the millisecond of the tap and the real navigation fetch 2 ms later, in the red one no `/notifications` request of any kind precedes the document GET 5 ms after the tap. On CI the shell (`data-chrome`, what `hydrated()` waits for) hydrates ~50 ms before the page's boundary, which holds the title bar and its bell (`PageHeader` is rendered by `page.tsx` inside the `loading.tsx` Suspense boundary); the test's tap lands ~90 ms after that gap and once landed inside it. **Cause:** React 19 makes one synchronous hydration attempt inside a click on a dehydrated boundary and, when it suspends (a chunk still loading), drops the event without `preventDefault` (`react-dom-client.production.js`, `dispatchEvent`), so the browser's own anchor default loaded `/notifications` as a new document; the head script's holder (§14.2 l) covered the back control, view links and tabs, never the bell. **Fix** (`8c7fc89`): the bell is the fourth held link: `HeaderBell` is a client component that marks itself live (`markLive`), the head script holds its tap and replays it (push; the 2 s fallback as for the others); unit tests for the held bell, its fallback and the live bell; `e2e/pre-hydration.spec.ts` proves the bell marks itself live and that a held bell tap is acknowledged within 100 ms and then made by the app in the same document. Not a test-side wait. Full CI on `phase-6` after the fix: run 37614966608.

Questions sent: (1) the rule format (editor rows "N minutes/hours/days before", 0 = Due now; the 1 h overdue reminder, the 24 h escalation and the event's 18:00 "tomorrow" fixed, outside the editor); (2) which before-due reminder is "the last" that is always emailed (the latest with N > 0); (3) do half days (leave or comp) pause reminders; (4) level 1 escalation when the creator is the Owner and there is no Admin step; (5) where each level is edited (task dialog, Settings → Task types, Templates, Settings → Thresholds for the org default); (6) reminders while `changes_requested` (keep firing). `end_day_reminder` folds into `reminders_tick` (engineering).
  **Owner answers (2026-10-02): yes to all six, plus:** (2) a reminder whose time has passed when the task is created or its deadline moved is skipped, never sent late; nothing replaces a skipped one. (3) "Pause" = **held**: a before-due or Due now reminder held by a full-day leave, comp leave or holiday goes at the start of the person's next working day (after quiet hours end) if the deadline is still ahead, as one combined message when several were held; past the deadline by then, it is dropped and the overdue reminder covers it; half days pause nothing; **weekly days off: the owner wrote "confirm weekly days off count as holidays here", which changes kickoff 5 decision 13 ("weekly offs pause nothing"): asked back.** (5) The dialog's Reminders is one collapsed line ("Reminders: 2 days before, 1 day before, when due") opening on tap, never taller by default; at most 5 rows a list; resolution task → template → task type → organisation, shown as "Using the default" until changed; if `/today` or `/my-day` would grow, lazy-load the editor and say so. **Email volume:** before building the send path, report the most always-emailed messages one task can produce, what the 20 / 90 caps drop and keep and where it is logged, and whether same-person reminders in one tick are combined; **escalations must be the last thing a cap drops.** `task_reminders` and the default schedule are created empty; **no backfill of reminders for existing production tasks without the owner's explicit OK.**

  - **The `task-settings.spec` reminders test's 30 s timeout (owner, 2026-10-02): a race in the test, not a slow screen.** Traced step by step: every step took 0.2 to 1.3 s (the slowest, opening the Type list on /tasks, 1.2 s); the test then waited out its timeout for "Discard this task?". `Escape` was pressed 30 ms after picking the type, while the Type list was still animating closed, so the list took it and the dialog never asked. Fix: the file's `pick()` waits for the listbox to be gone (no timeout raised, no retry; no other spec presses Escape straight after a pick). - **Slice 7, the Owner's daily digest (`digest_daily`): done, reviewed (2026-10-03).** **Owner decisions (2026-10-03, binding):** (1) **content**, until 6.5 builds the end-of-day report, only counts the app already has, each a line that taps through to its screen: **attendance yesterday** (present, on leave, absent, day not ended; the absent and day-not-ended named, at most 5 by name, then "+N more"); **tasks** (approved yesterday, overdue now, waiting for the Owner's approval); **requests waiting for the Owner** (leave, expense claims); **emails held back yesterday by the daily limit, by kind** (only above 0); **no money amounts of any kind** (an email can be forwarded or read on a shared screen; expense claims a count only); **lines at 0 left out; all 0 → the one line "Nothing needs you today."; never skipped**: one digest every day (weekly days off included), to the **Owner only**. (2) **Email only**: no bell entry, no push, nothing in Alerts or the unread count; on the always-email list (quiet hours don't apply); **counts against the caps as an ordinary email** (`email_daily_cap_org - 10` and the per-person cap) **but is processed before the other ordinary emails of its run** (escalations still first); always its own email, never in the per-person batch. (3) **Unreachable people: not in this slice** (5.4 adds them; no placeholder line). (4) **A rendered sample on the branch preview** (staging data) before the phone walk. (5) **The 08:00 trigger is a pg_cron job, not a Worker cron** (owner change 2026-10-03): the Worker's crons do not run on branch previews, so the send could not be proved before staging; a Worker cron would change production's Worker config; and the Cloudflare account's cron-trigger count is shared with staging and the website. So `wrangler.jsonc`, `worker/index.js` and the cron routes are untouched. (6) `/diagnostics/digest` answers 404 on production **decided at runtime**, never sends or writes, and is the Owner's only.

  **Built:** migration **`20261003015912_owner_digest`** (expand-only): `notification_kinds.in_app` (default true) and the kind `owner_digest` (not actionable, always emailed, not in-app); the `notifications_select` policy re-created to hide a not-in-app kind from the API role (so the bell, its count, `notifications_inbox` and Realtime never see it; every app read path is RLS: `core/notifications/inbox.ts`, the inbox function is security invoker); `app.owner_digest_payload(org, now)` (counts, names, held back by kind; service_role only), `app.owner_digest_text(payload)` (the row's plain-text body), `public.digest_daily(p_now)` (service_role only; per organisation under an advisory lock; one row per IST day for `app.org_owner_id`, written **directly, not through `app.notify()`, so no push delivery**, `read_at` = its time, link `/today`, title "Your morning summary · Sat 3 Oct"), **pg_cron `digest_daily` `30 2 * * *`** (`select public.digest_daily(now())`), `public.owner_digest_preview()` (authenticated; FORBIDDEN for anyone but the org's Owner; reads only) and `email_claim` re-created (same signature, a `payload` column added at the end; the digest taken into a run first, grouped alone, ordered after escalations and before other ordinary groups, capped as ordinary; everything else identical: pgTAP 45 and 52 pass unchanged). `core/notifications/digest-content.ts` (zod-checked payload, `digestSections`, `renderDigestEmail` in the shared shell, `emailDocument()` extracted from `email-content.ts` with byte-identical output, `digestSamplePage`), the dispatcher renders kind `owner_digest` from its payload (a payload that is not a digest's → `failed` / `invalid_payload` at once, reported, the run goes on), `digest-preview.ts` (the Owner's read), `digest-diagnostic.ts` (the runtime guard) and `app/diagnostics/digest/route.ts`. Docs: DATA-MODEL §0/§9, WORKFLOWS §8 (`digest_daily | pg_cron | 08:00 IST (02:30 UTC)`), the email policy, ceilings and digest bullets, PERMISSIONS (notifications), ARCHITECTURE §9.
  **Interpretations (the session's; the owner may correct them):** IST everywhere; "yesterday" = the IST day before the digest's. Attendance = `attendance_days` of yesterday of **active** members: present / on leave (leave, half_day, comp_leave) / absent by the day's status, where **a day still `pending_review` counts by what it stands at, `coalesce(submitted_choice, final_status)`** (the brief said "by its final_status", but a submitted day has `final_status` null until the Owner decides, so a Start day waiting for approval would never count as present; this is the expression `attendance_decide` approves and Start day reads); day not ended = `end_not_recorded`. **Every expected member has a row by 08:00:** the 23:59 `absent_check` writes a leave-derived or proposed-absent day for every active permanent member with attendance on a working day (7-day catch-up); a day off has rows only for those who started a day, and freelancers have none. Approved yesterday = `completed_at` in yesterday, not archived; overdue now = not completed / cancelled, not archived, `due_at` before now; waiting for the Owner = `task_counts().to_decide` for the Owner (admin-approved, or submitted with the Owner as approver and not an assignee; not filtered by archive, as task_counts). Leave waiting = `countPendingRequests` (submitted, not the attendance prompt's); expense claims = `countPendingClaims` (submitted). Held back = email deliveries `skipped_cap` created yesterday, by kind, labelled in TS ("Overdue reminders", "Task assigned"…; else the kind's description; else "Other emails"). **Links (accepted by the owner 2026-10-03, with one change):** present, on leave and day not ended → `/reports/month?month=<yesterday's month>` (no screen shows one past day for everyone; the team's month is where yesterday's days count, a person's row opens their days); **absent → `/approvals`** (owner: where the proposed absences wait; Approvals has no attendance view, its Attendance group comes first); counting a waiting day by the person's choice: accepted; approved yesterday → `/tasks/all?state=completed` (the Completed filter; no "yesterday" filter exists); overdue → `/tasks/all?overdue=overdue`; waiting for approval, leave, expense claims → `/approvals`; held back → **no link** (no screen shows them).

Code: `core/notifications/reachability.ts` (labels, row parsing, the report's zod), `reachability-store.ts` (the two RPCs), `app-report-actions.ts` (`reportAppOpen`, no revalidation), `push/app-report.ts` (once per open) loaded by `PushSync` with `import()`, `appReportOf` in `push/browser.ts`, `env.ts` `operationalWarning()`, `components/reachability-list.tsx`, the screen `app/(app)/settings/notifications/` (page + tracing loading), a Settings row for both roles, `digest-content.ts` (the line; an old payload reads as 0). Seed: per-project `reach-admin-*` / `reach-staff-*`, and every seeded person recorded as already alerted (else the first hourly run would put ~115 alerts in the local Owner's Alerts in the middle of an e2e run).
  **Interpretations (the session's; the owner may correct them):** (a) **`failing` = every active device has 2 or more consecutive errors** (one error is not "repeated"; a success resets the count; the dispatcher's retries take a broken device to 5 and `expired` within about 80 minutes), or the latest disabled device `expired`; **`ok` = any active device with fewer than 2** (one working device is enough, as for the banner). (b) Order: ok → failing (active) → the app report says iOS and not installed → the latest disabled device `gone` (permission revoked) → `expired` (failing) → no report and the latest sign-in from an iPhone/iPad/iPod (`session_events.user_agent`, the request's User-Agent header; an iPad presenting as a Mac is not seen there) → no subscription. A device disabled as `deactivated`/`signed_out` counts as none. (c) **First login = `members.joined_at`** (stamped when the invite is accepted, at the first sign-in). (d) **At the release, the first hourly run alerts the Owner once about every tracked person who is not reachable and joined more than 48 h ago** (one always-emailed alert each, counted against the ordinary email cap; batched into one email per run for the Owner): that is the rule as written. (e) The Owner is tracked and listed on the screen ("Everyone", and in "Can't be reached" when it applies) but never alerted about and **left out of the digest's count**. (f) The screen reads the state **live**; the alert and the digest read the hourly job's record (at most an hour behind). (g) "Open task" = not completed or cancelled and not archived; an Admin's people are the active (not removed) assignees and a freelancer assignee's current coordinator, the Admin included when they are an assignee themselves. (h) The Owner's platform is the app report's, else the most recently seen subscription's; "last success" the latest `last_success_at` of any of their subscriptions. (i) The alert's body: "<reason> since <date>." (e.g. "Notifications never turned on since 1 Oct."), no entity (so it never collapses with another record's run), payload `{member_id, state}`; `alerted_at` is kept across state changes, so a person is alerted at most once a week however their state moves. (j) The operational warning also names the three `VAPID_*` when all are set but push is still off (malformed), names only. (k) **The digest line sits under its own heading "People", after "Requests waiting for you" and before the held-back emails** (both are about notifications reaching people). (l) Staff get no screen (their own devices stay on Me).
  **The banner (5.2) and the Owner's own states:** the banner shows while the member has **no active subscription** (`endpoints.length === 0` in the layout). It covers `no_subscription`, `permission_revoked` (the device went `gone`), `ios_not_installed` (its "Install MaxOff" form) and `failing` once the device is `expired`. **It does not show for `failing` while the devices are still active with 2 to 4 consecutive errors** (the banner believes an active row works): a window of at most ~80 minutes per failed push before the device is disabled, not redesigned here, for the owner to decide.

(c) **Copy per reason**: off "Notifications are off · Turn on", blocked "Notifications are blocked · Fix", install as before, failing "Notifications aren't reaching you · Fix". On the device in hand an iPhone in Safari always reads "install"; a device that can turn notifications on is never told to install. The failing and unconfirmed sheets send a test (from any device). (d) **A test the push service accepted now sets `last_success_at`** (and the error count to 0): needed for decision 1's "a test or a real one", and decision 6's "accepted counts as working". (g) **The welcome screen's iOS step count comes from the request's user agent** (server-side); the step itself settles once the browser is read (`data-ready`). (h) **The walkthrough finishes on its own test step only**, not on a test from the band or Me's Help row. (i) **Remove deletes the row** (as Sign out of this device does); **a removed device that is still signed in and opened again with permission granted turns itself back on** (PushSync's re-subscribe, 5.2): see the open question. (k) The device list's second line is "<platform> · installed app | browser". (l) "Did it arrive?" shows after an accepted test on Me's Help row and at the walkthrough's end, not in the band's sheet (the band closes on success). (m) Remove is a red-outline (`destructive`) button; its confirmation's red button names the device.

Remove's rule is kept (own rows only; anyone else's NOT_FOUND, the Owner included; a row already removed NOT_FOUND). **(2) Any sign-in lands on the welcome screen** while the member's walkthrough is unfinished (`signInLanding` in `core/auth/paths.ts`; `login` and the reset-password `setPassword` use it); the login form no longer sends `installedIphone` (removed, with its schema field). **(3) Any test a device received finishes the walkthrough:** `sendTestPush` calls `onboarding_finish('test')` after `recordTestDelivered` when a push service accepted it (the walkthrough, the band, Me → Help); the walkthrough's card no longer calls `finishOnboarding('test')` itself. **(4) Any delivery on record ends the band:** checked, nothing to fix: `app.push_band` reads `last_success_at`, which the dispatcher's real `push_subscription_result(id,'sent')` sets exactly as a test does (since v1.3.0, migration `20260930075159_push_dispatch`); proved in pgTAP 60. **The staging count:** `public.push_band_census()` → `(people, devices)`: active members whose `app.push_band` is `'unconfirmed'`, and their active devices; read-only (stable), service_role only; the dispatch option **`push-band-count`** in `.github/workflows/preview.yml` (staging-only job, the `notifications-removal-dry-run` shape, the same project-ref guard, output to the step summary).     **Interpretations (the session's; the owner may correct them):** (a) a removed device **leaves Me's device list** (as when the row was deleted); its row is kept only for the marker. (b) **"Sign out of this device" on a removed device keeps the marker** (the row is not deleted), so signing in there again does not turn it back on either. (c) **Another person signing in on the same browser can still turn it on for themselves** (their automatic subscribe takes the disabled row over, as before): the marker is the remover's, not the device's for everyone. (d) **A browser that replaces its subscription by itself** (the push service rotates it; `pushsubscriptionchange`) gets a new endpoint, which the server cannot tie to the removed row: it comes back on. Rare; listed under Ideas. (e) **`next` still wins** (as the installed-iPhone rule did): a sign-in that asked for a safe `next` (the proxy adds one when a signed-out person opens a deep link; plain `/` adds none) goes there; any other sign-in of an unfinished joiner lands on `/me?welcome=1`. (f) **A reset password is a sign-in too**: `setPassword` for an active member uses the same rule. (g) **A real notification delivered does not finish the walkthrough** (only a test does, as asked); the band ends with either. (h) The census's **devices** = the active devices of the counted people (each of them has no working delivery on record); **people** = active members only (an invited person with a device row is not counted).

- **Phase 5 review: background calls (2026-10-06, Opus 5.5; uncommitted for the owner's review):** **Cause** of CI run 37118131079 (`notifications.spec.ts:185`, the tap on a task never navigated; Ideas (j)): Next queues a server action sent while a navigation is in flight behind it, sets the router state to the action's pending result once the navigation's data arrives, and React does not commit the new page until the action answers. The live bell's `readUnreadCount`, sent on the Realtime channel's first join, went out just after the tapped task's data arrived; the spec held every action, so the task never opened. Any action the app sent by itself could do this to a person on a slow connection (owner decision: a background call must never stop a page from opening). An action sent *before* a navigation is pre-empted by it, which is why 27 local runs never hit it. **Fix (ARCHITECTURE §4.4):** no server action is ever sent without a tap: every background call is a plain `fetch` (`core/http/background.ts`, never rejects, a `Result` back) to a route handler (`core/http/background-route.ts`: Origin check on POSTs, 401 without an active member, then the module's own zod + permission + repository, a `Result` with its status, `no-store`). Converted: `reportAppOpen` → `POST /api/app-report`; `subscribePush` (the automatic re-subscribe) → `POST /api/push/subscription` (the service worker's route, now with the Origin check, Ideas "Phase 5 review" (h)); `readUnreadCount` (first join, confirmations) → `GET /api/notifications/unread`; `markRecordRead` → `POST /api/notifications/read-record`; Chat's `markTaskRead` → `POST /api/tasks/read`; the task dialog's `loadAvailability` → `GET /api/tasks/availability`; the Owner's dialogs' `getClientHandover`, `getCoordinatorChoices`, `getFreelancerHandover`, `getOpenTaskCount` → `GET /api/team/{client-handover,coordinator-choices,freelancer-handover,open-task-count}`. The old actions are gone (their logic is plain `server-only` functions in `core/notifications/background.ts`, `modules/{tasks,team}/actions/background.ts`, through the modules' `index.ts`). **Lint:** `maxoff/no-background-action` (an action called or handed on inside an effect's or a timer's callback; it caught 9 of the 11 old call sites, the other two were a `useCallback` and a lazily imported module function, which the ARCHITECTURE rule covers). **Proof:** `notifications.spec` "a background call sent while a page opens never holds it…" (fails on the old build; passes now); `:185` now holds every background call, not only actions; `view-address.spec`'s report test now proves the address is written at once while the report is out; `reachability.spec` counts `POST /api/app-report`; `task-page.spec` waits for background calls as for actions. **Left as actions, on purpose:** a tap's own continuation (an upload's `completeUpload` / `failUpload`, then the dialog's submit) while its dialog holds the screen.

PR #34 is the owner's to merge. v1.3.0's production migrations run through `scripts/production-migrations.sh` (owner-approved).

**Reads never reload (owner decision 2026-10-01, Ideas (a); and Ideas (h); Opus 5.5, cloud session; branch `phase-5`).** Status: **done**, pushed. Marking notifications read never refreshes or reloads the page:
- **No read revalidates:** `markRecordRead`, `markNotificationRead`, `markAllNotificationsRead` (they answer `{ marked, writtenAt }`, the server's clock after the write) and Chat's `markTaskRead`; `/open?n=` already marked during its own render. Unit `notifications-center/tests/read-actions.test.ts`.
- **The bell drops on the device:** `core/notifications/read-receipts.ts` (pure, unit `read-receipts.test.ts`: issue, written, a count made in flight, Mark all read, failure restored by the next count, rows) + `send-read.ts` (`sendRead`, never throws, no toast). The server's count carries **when it was counted** (`readUnread()` → `{ count, countedAt }`, stamped before the query; the layout passes it as `unread` to `AppShell`); every spot that draws it subtracts the reads not yet in it: the title bar's bell, `UnreadNavCount` (Staff's Alerts tab, the desktop bell, More; in `bell-count.tsx`), Alerts' `UnreadSummary` and rows (`NotificationRow`, `data-unread` follows; dot and bold by `group-data-[unread]/row`). Opening a record: `RecordReadReceipt` (server, in `<Suspense fallback={null}>` on the task page and the client and person layouts) counts the unread rows about it (`countUnreadAbout`) and sends nothing when there are none; `/open?n=` drops it through `ReadRecorded`.
- **Confirmation:** the live bell's UPDATE events for the member's own reads (in flight or within `OWN_READ_QUIET_MS`) call `readUnreadCount` (count only, no revalidation) instead of `router.refresh()`; refresh on return / rejoin / navigation fallbacks unchanged. A failed write stops counting at the next server count.
- **Task lists:** `markTaskRead` no longer revalidates; the Tasks tab, all tasks and Approvals re-read themselves when shown again from the router's cache after Chat was read (`TasksFreshOnReturn`, `taskReads`, `core/lib/drawn-since.ts`, which `live-state.ts` now uses too).
- **Proof:** `notifications.spec` "opening a task reads its rows in the background…" (desktop, 375, 430): the receipt is held until two views were tapped, the bell has already dropped, no load event, the window marker survives, **no action answer carries `x-action-revalidated`**, the count stays after a reload. On the old build it fails (the bell, and 3 revalidating answers); the old build did **not** hard-reload in this held sequence (the reload needs a timing this spec cannot force), so the revalidation header is what it pins. `notifications.spec`'s two `unreadOf` checks after a tap now poll (the screen shows the read before the write lands).
- **Ideas (h):** cause found and the spec fixed (see Ideas (h)).
- **Numbers:** `task-page.spec --repeat-each=16`, desktop + mobile: before **5 failed / 255 passed** (`:570` 5 of 16 on mobile: 4 the band gap (h), 1 a `goBack` aborted by a reload after Chat's revalidating `markTaskRead`); after **0 failed / 260 passed** on the final tree (two earlier after-runs, before the last two fixes: 1 and 7 failed; the 7 were mostly the spec's action tracker waiting on a `readUnreadCount` the old document sent as a reload or a back across a full load replaced it: such a request never answers and Chromium reports no failure, so the tracker now forgets the old document's requests at `domcontentloaded`; and the live bell now asks for the count only while a read of the device still waits for one, `readsUnconfirmed`). Targeted: notifications, push, loading-screens, shell, pull-to-refresh 166 passed / 20 skipped. `pnpm check` green (unit 1312, pgTAP 3403 in 50 files). **Budget:** `/today` and `/my-day` at 784.9 of 785.0 KB (the count's client piece); the next client byte on the shell needs a budget decision.

**5A review fix pass (2026-10-01, after the combined architecture + security review of 5A; Opus 5.5, cloud session; branch `phase-5`).** Status: **done**, pushed (`ee93678`, `c6d2c06`, docs); the dispatched full CI run is the authority. What changed, by review item:
- **M1 (one bad subscription stalled push):** `sendWebPush` never throws (endpoint check, encryption, VAPID and fetch each caught, `detail` a code: `endpoint_refused`, `encrypt_failed`, `vapid_failed`, `timeout`, `network_error`, `HTTP n`); `runPushDispatch` catches per item (recorded `retry` `dispatch_error`, so it fails at the last backoff) and per device result; the cron route runs the email pass even when the push pass throws (500 with both reports). `fitPayload` cuts the body with "…" until the JSON fits one record (title, link, tag untouched). Keys strict in zod (`push/schemas.ts`) and in SQL (`app.push_key_valid`): base64url, p256dh 65 bytes starting 0x04, auth 16. Unit: `dispatcher.test.ts` (poisoned device beside a good one, poisoned only device, a throwing store, the long body, a timeout), `route.test.ts` (email still runs), `env.push.test.ts` (schema).
- **M2 (SSRF / fan-out):** migration **`20261001053934_review_5a_fixes`**: INSERT and UPDATE on `push_subscriptions` revoked from `authenticated` (DELETE kept, own rows; the app uses the RPCs only: subscribe, re-subscribe and sign-out checked; the e2e helpers write as the service role); the upsert takes https on a public DNS name only (`app.push_endpoint_valid`: no IP literal, no localhost/.local/.internal), http on the loopback host only while **`app.local_flags`** holds `push_loopback_endpoints` (written by `supabase/seed.sql` only: never staging or production); **10 active subscriptions a member: the 11th disables the least recently seen other one as `expired`** (never refused: a new phone must work). Defence in depth: `pushEndpointAllowed` in the sender (https + DNS name; loopback http only with `PUSH_ALLOW_LOOPBACK_ENDPOINTS=1`, set by `playwright.config.ts` for the e2e server, ignored when `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ENV` is staging/production; `.env.example`). pgTAP **`47_review_5a_fixes`** (49: per role direct INSERT/UPDATE denied, RPC allowed, `app.local_flags` unreachable, keys, endpoints, the switch, the cap, the cooldown, the Crew messages); `40`, `41`, `42`, `45` and `00` updated for the revoked writes and the strict keys (fixture keys from `pg_temp.p256` / `pg_temp.auth16`).
- **M3 (owner decision 29):** the cron and dispatch tests moved to **`e2e/push-cron.spec.ts`** and, with `push-quiet.spec.ts`, into the serial project **`push-cron`** (1 worker, after `owner-bulk`; `ci.yml` shard 2 runs it alone after owner-bulk with `--no-deps`). `ownTheDispatchQueue()` (`e2e/push-shared.ts`) closes every waiting push/email delivery as `failed` `e2e_cleared` and gives every recent notification without an email row a closed one; quiet hours set once. Each step dispatches once and asserts its own row; `drainDispatch` and the until-sent loop are gone; `push.spec.ts` no longer runs the cron nor touches `org_settings`.
- **M4:** `member_invite`, `app.coordinator_eligible` (x2) and `comp_leave_grant` re-created from their latest bodies with "Crew" (pgTAP 47 asserts each DETAIL).
- **S1:** `AbortSignal.timeout(10_000)` on the push fetch and the Resend fetch; a timeout is a retryable error (unit tests).
- **S2:** `push_test_claim()` (SQL, row-locked): RATE_LIMITED "A test was sent a moment ago. Try again in half a minute." while any active row was tested under 30 s ago, else stamps `last_test_at`; `sendTestPush` claims before sending (`actions.test.ts`). No e2e taps twice.
- **S3:** superseded by the v1.3.0 production migration step, **done 2026-10-01**: `scripts/production-migrations.sh` (pushed) and the `deploy.yml` change (a separate local commit, **not pushed: waits for the owner**). See "Things the next session must know".
- **S4 (`task-page.spec:631`, and `:509` the same way):** cause confirmed from the traces of a `--repeat-each=16` run: the bell's **`markRecordRead`** (and Chat's `markTaskRead`) answer with a revalidation; when one lands after a view switch (`history.replaceState`), Next 16 **hard-reloads the page** at the current URL (a document GET of `?tab=details` in the trace), closing the Chat sheet or resetting the view under the spec. The spec now tracks the page's server actions and lets them answer before it measures (`receiptsSettled`, in `live()`, after Chat opens and after each test), and polls the tab heights; the fixture's own notifications are read before the page opens (`fixtureNotificationsRead`: no receipt, so no Realtime echo re-reading the screen mid-measurement). **`--repeat-each=16` on desktop + mobile: before 4 failed / 252 passed (`:631` 2, `:509` 2, both the reload); after 1 failed / 255 passed, twice: `:631` 0 of 32, and `:509` once each run at its sticky-band check (gap 101–104 px, no reload in the trace: a separate flake, Ideas (h)).** **The same reload can happen to a person** (tap a view within a second of opening a task with unread notifications): Ideas / tech debt.

**5A bell (2026-10-01, owner cut (b), the simplest form; Opus 5.5, cloud session; branch `phase-5-bell` from `origin/phase-5`, merged with it, never pushed to `phase-5`; no PR, no merge).** Built, targeted e2e green locally; **the dispatched full CI run is the authority** (not yet read).

- **Built:** the bell's unread count (`countUnread()`, `core/notifications/inbox.ts`) in the layout's streamed nav counts (`alerts`: Staff's Alerts tab, the desktop top bar's bell, and the title bar's bell through `BellCountProvider`, because `PageHeader` is also drawn inside client components); Alerts `/notifications` (`modules/notifications-center`: newest first, 20 a page, "N unread" + Mark all read (neutral outline; the screen has no red action), pager below, empty state, traced `loading.tsx`); a row with a link opens through `/open?n=<id>` (the entry marks it read and opens the row's own link: one request, works before hydration), a row with none is read by the tap; back re-reads a list drawn before a change (`FreshOnReturn`: the router restores it from its cache otherwise, found by the spec); opening a task, a client or a person marks its rows read (`MarkRecordRead` → `notifications_mark_read`); the live bell (`LiveUpdates`, lazy, in the layout: Realtime `postgres_changes` on the member's rows → `router.refresh()`, guarded like refresh on return; the token handed over by the server, `createRealtimeSupabase` with supabase-js's `accessToken`, joined only after `setAuth()`: joining first joined as `anon` and every event came as a 401; a new token is fetched just after expiry). The "coming soon" Alerts stand-in is gone (`stand-ins.ts`, `mobile.spec`). ARCHITECTURE §10, §14.2 h and §7a, DATA-MODEL §9, WORKFLOWS §9a, README (seeded `alerts-<role>-<project>`) updated.
- **CI:** `ci.yml`'s e2e job starts Realtime (no `-x realtime`); global setup waits for a channel to join.
- **Proofs:** `e2e/notifications.spec.ts` (desktop, 375, 430: order, unread, count, tap → record over its list → back to the list then Alerts, read in place, Mark all read, empty; **Realtime through the hold proxy**: a new row and a read on another device reach the open screen with no reload; **RLS on Realtime**: two members subscribed with no filter of their own each hear only their own rows, and a connection with no member token hears no row; an Admin's bell matches their unread rows and opening a person reads the row about them; large text 130/200%; installed back at 375/430 for Staff's tab and the Admin's title-bar bell); `loading-screens.spec` `/notifications` per role; `mobile.spec` `/notifications` per role; unit (`live-rules`, `live-state`, `when`, deep-link).
- **Owner's asks answered:** a notification row's columns are `title`, `body`, `link`, `entity`/`entity_id`, `payload`, `actor_id`, `kind`, times: written only by `app.notify()` under decisions 24 (no amount anywhere; pgTAP 40/41/43) and 25 (a person who lost access gets no entity, link or ids), and Realtime sends a member only what the API already lets them read (their own rows). **Two Supabase behaviours to know:** (1) a **DELETE** event is not RLS-checked by Realtime and carries the row's id alone (notifications are never deleted outside the local fixtures); (2) a connection with only the publishable key may subscribe and **hears that a change happened (no row, a 401 notice)**; the hosted Realtime setting "allow only private channels" would refuse such joins (Ideas).

The container restarted meanwhile: **Docker must be started by hand** (`nohup dockerd >/tmp/dockerd.log 2>&1 &`; the Supabase containers come back on their own) and the container ran ~50% slower afterwards. **`pnpm test:e2e -- <files>` runs the whole suite** (pnpm forwards the literal `--`); `pnpm test:e2e <files>` filters. The unit's diff is `1571299..HEAD` (the local `main` was stale until the orchestrator reset it to `origin/main`). A stale `next-server` from an e2e run can linger: stop it before the next run. **`pkill -f <pattern>` kills the calling shell when its own command line contains the pattern** (and `next-server` also matches Supabase Studio's container, which restarts itself): stop a leftover app server by PID.

'!.claude/**'`) or remove the worktree first; (4) the owner's standing instruction (above) routes day-to-day decisions to the advisor's messages and `ADVISOR` comments on #49; (5) `scripts/keep-awake.ps1` is Windows-only, not used in the cloud; (6) subagents here are cut at the account's limits (session, then weekly: Fable resets 02:00 UTC): brief them to commit at every green slice and keep the handoff current.

Any later change is a **new migration file**, never an edit (the 6B case: `12e5432` edited `20261007130000_eod_report_weekly_digest.sql` after `f097765` had applied it; fixed 2026-10-08 in `fa66044`: the file restored to its `f097765` bytes, the revoke in the new migration `20261008020456_eod_reports_grants`; push run 37716501209 green incl. pgTAP, Preview 37716501206 applied it to staging).

Since decision 24 the Owner's Today has no "Everyone's in." and its risks section is hidden when empty ("Nothing overdue." no longer shows); the one empty line is "Nothing else needs you today."
- **6A mechanics (read before touching Today, My Day, the work report or `LiveUpdates`):** (1) **PostgREST answers at most 1000 rows** (`supabase/config.toml` `max_rows`, and the same on hosted projects): `member_availability()` without `member_ids` answered 2,667 rows on the e2e database and was cut silently (the Admin's leave Issue vanished); `listLeaveDays(from, to, memberIds)` reads slices that stay under it, and `listKpiFacts` reads page by page. Any new read that can pass 1000 rows does the same. (2) **Realtime does not check a DELETE against RLS** (it carries the id alone): the day channel listens to INSERT and UPDATE only; the RLS e2e does too (a fixture reset in another project's run was heard as an empty DELETE). (3) **The full people board moved to `/today/people`**: specs that tapped a `board-row` on `/today` now open `/today/people` (motion, pre-hydration, tap-feedback, owner-review, working-day); Today's people rows are `needs-you-person`. (4) **Seed:** `day-staff-<project>` and `day-admin-<project>` (`day-local-password`) are `e2e/dashboards.spec.ts`'s own Crew member and Admin; the file runs serially per project; the Owner is shared, so its checks look for their own rows. (5) **Stand-ins:** only the Calendar is left (`STAND_INS.calendar`, 6.4); `dayStandIn` is gone. (6) Today's risk reads run only for the key that may read them (`can()` before the RPC), so a role change hides a row instead of breaking the screen. (7) **A lazily loaded menu's stand-in takes a press as Radix's trigger does, and `AfterPage` imports at idle** (`back-gesture.spec:479` [mobile], CI runs 37575602126 and 37587912609). First fix (`a494548`): the stand-ins open on pointerdown, as Radix does (`menuStandInPress`, which also prevents the default as Radix does when it opens). It was red again: **cause found with the CPU slowed 8–16× (CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`)**: the shell was hydrated (`html[data-chrome]`) but /me's own part was not; the tap made React hydrate it inside the tap, the `AfterPage` effect of that hydration imported the theme menu (already loaded by the top bar's copy, so at once), and the stand-in was swapped for the real menu between the capture and the bubble of that one pointerdown: the stand-in's handler never ran, the menu stayed closed. Measured, tap after `hydrated(page)`: origin/main 14/16 open (its 2 misses are `hydrated()` timing out at 16×), 6A before the fix 4/16, after (`AfterPage` imports at idle) 16/16. A tap **before** hydration reaches no handler on either build (same steps without the wait: main 5/12 open, 6A 7/12): the spec now waits for `hydrated(page)` like the others, and the new spec "a menu tapped while its screen is still hydrating opens on that tap" (CPU 8×; red 4/4 without the idle start) guards the regression. (8) **The day screens' re-read has its own timer, dropped when the screen is left** (`LiveUpdates`' dashboard effect): it used to share the bell's `refreshSoon` timer, so a change heard on My Day just before a tap re-read the screen the tap moved to (proof: `dashboards.spec` "a change heard just before leaving My Day never re-reads the screen it moved to", red on the old build with two `/leave` screen fetches). The timer also re-checks the address when it fires. `dashboards.spec` "a tap while My Day re-reads…" holds a re-read at the network and taps the bell: Next moves softly (green on both builds; it guards the class). CI run 37578637275's red (`notifications.spec:754` [mobile-lg], a full load of `/notifications` on the bell tap) is **not explained**: its trace has a router re-prefetch at +765 ms and a `/my-day` fetch at +795 ms, 264 ms after the document, too early for any `LiveUpdates` re-read (a join, an event and 400 ms); 34 local repeats are green; watch for it (Ideas).
- **6B mechanics (read before touching the calendar, the end-of-day report, the digests or the Owner's Today):** (1) **The role-label sweep (`tests/role-label-sweep.test.ts`) refuses the literal `"crew"` anywhere but `ROLE_LABELS`**: a scope or enum value for the third role is `"staff"` (the calendar's `CalendarScope`). (2) **`member_availability()` answers only timed events** (`event_start_at` set) and only non-Owner members, 62 days at most, under the 1000-row limit: the calendar reads it in slices (`listAvailability`), and a date-only event of someone else has no Busy block. (3) **pgTAP cannot run here** (no Docker): 64 was written blind and proved by CI's push run; a timestamptz inside a payload compares as `to_jsonb(ts) #>> '{}'`, never `ts::text`; a notification's `created_at` set in the future (a job run at `today_at('08:00')`) is not queued by `email_claim(now())`, so claim at the row's own moment. (4) **The hand-edited `database.types.ts`** must be regenerated by the next session with a local stack (`pnpm db:types`); any drift shows as a typecheck error. (5) **The notify icon generator needs a browser:** `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers node scripts/generate-notify-icons.mjs` works in the cloud container (Chromium is bundled there); it rewrites every group's PNG, so restore the unchanged ones from git after adding a group. (6) **A static render with the build's CSS** (`.next/static/chunks/*.css` inlined into a hand-written page, screenshotted with that Chromium) is enough to check a component's box math at 375 and 430 when no database is at hand (the bell badge, the Owner's card); the scripts are in the 6B report, not the repo. (7) **The Owner's Today hides empty sections** (decision 24): an e2e that needs a section makes its data first (`dashboards.spec` creates a task due today for "Today's tasks"); the loading trace's settled point is `[data-slot="owner-today"] > :first-child`. (8) **The calendar's auto view** renders both Day (below `md`) and Week (from `md`) from one week's data: a selector for "the switcher" must take the visible copy (`[data-slot="calendar-view"]:visible`). (9) **`digest_daily` is unscheduled, not dropped:** pgTAP 55 still proves the function; `/diagnostics/digest` shows the weekly now.
- **The first-load JS budget of `/today` and `/my-day` is 670 KB since 6.0 (2026-10-07; measured 632.0 / 630.8 KB after 6A). Never raised: new code on these routes is a server component or loads after the page (`AfterPage` in the shell and for a closed dialog or menu; `next/dynamic` with server rendering in its own `<Suspense>` for an interactive block, as Today's approvals preview, `today/approvals-preview.tsx`). A loading screen imports no `"use client"` file for a skeleton (the 6A `ApprovalGroupSkeleton` leak). A new module run only for its side effects goes into `package.json` `sideEffects` (ARCHITECTURE §19).** History: **The first-load JS budget of `/today` and `/my-day` is 792 KB (owner decision 2026-10-03: raised from 790 KB, "bundler regrouping only, 2026-10-03, proven: no reminder code in either first-load set"). It was the last raise without ROADMAP 6.0, the first-load diet that lowers it again. Until then, any real new code on these routes' first load is lazy-loaded, no exceptions; an overage caused only by regrouping is shown to the owner with before and after numbers, never raised by a session.** Earlier: **790 KB (owner decision 2026-10-01), and the spare ~4 KB is not headroom for features.** The reload fix (the held pre-hydration tap and the named slide, ARCHITECTURE §14.2 j/l) added ~1 KB and the two routes measured 786.0 KB against 785 KB, so both were raised to 790 KB in `bundle-budget.json` (on the v1.3.1 fix branch `fix/no-surprise-reloads`). The owner's note, exactly: **"the spare ~4 KB is not headroom for features; phase 6's Today / My Day sections load in their own lazy or streamed chunks and may not raise this budget without an owner decision."**
- **5A review fixes, mechanics (2026-10-01):** (1) **Members never write `push_subscriptions` directly** (INSERT/UPDATE revoked): `push_subscription_upsert` is the way in; a spec or pgTAP fixture that needs a device writes as the service role/postgres, with keys in the browser's shape (`pg_temp.p256` / `pg_temp.auth16` in pgTAP; `generateReceiverKeys()` in e2e). (2) **`app.local_flags` is local-only** (the seed writes `push_loopback_endpoints`); a hosted database never has it, so http push endpoints are refused there. A pgTAP file that needs the opposite deletes the row inside its transaction. (3) **Specs that run the cron dispatch live in the serial `push-cron` project** (`e2e/push-cron.spec.ts`, `push-quiet.spec.ts`), start with `ownTheDispatchQueue()` and dispatch once per step; never add a dispatch spec to the parallel projects. (4) **`task-page.spec.ts` waits for the page's server actions** (`receiptsSettled`) before measuring: a background receipt answering after a view switch reloads the page.
- **v1.3.0 production migration step (2026-10-01; `scripts/production-migrations.sh`, `tests/production-migrations.test.ts`):** production is at **v1.2.0 = `main` `dd087cc`** (phase 4 + the Crew label; v1.2.1 added no migration), newest migration `20260930070616_phase4_review_fixes`. 5A ships as **v1.3.0**, and its `20260930050132` and `20260930073623` sort **before** that, so a plain `supabase db push` refuses ("--include-all"; reproduced locally). The script lists production's migrations; **fails** when the listing is unreadable or when production holds a version the tag lacks (production only ever receives released tags: no stand-ins, ever); skips when nothing is pending; otherwise prints the pending versions and runs `supabase migration up --linked --include-all` (only the missing files, version order, one transaction each, recorded as `db push` records them), then lists again and fails if anything is still pending. Never `migration repair`, never an edit or revert. `DRY_RUN_LIST` and `MIGRATIONS_TARGET=local` as in the staging script. **Proof (local, 2026-10-01):** the local DB reset from a worktree at `dd087cc` with its seed (45 migrations, newest `070616`); `MIGRATIONS_TARGET=local` applied exactly `050132`, `073623`, `075159`, `180227`, `001614`, `003242`, `003253`, `003353`, `053934` in that order, then nothing pending; its schema (`pg_dump --schema-only` of `public` + `app`, `pg_get_functiondef` of every function, policies, RLS flags, table/column/function grants, triggers, the publication, cron jobs, the migration history) is **identical** to a fresh `pnpm db:reset`, the five task functions byte-identical (both phase 4's fixes and the notify calls, from `180227`); pgTAP on the out-of-order database 3400/3403, the 3 failures the seed's `push_loopback_endpoints` flag that the v1.2.0 seed lacks (as production lacks it). **Window:** from `050132` to `180227` the five task functions run 5A's first bodies without phase 4's review fixes (the 20-assignee and reminder bounds, decision 34's coordinator rules); locally 0.2 s, on production a few seconds; no cron calls them, and the old Worker is still serving. **`deploy.yml`:** the production job's "Apply migrations to the production database" step runs `bash scripts/production-migrations.sh` (same env); committed **locally only**, as the last commit, for the owner to approve and push. Until it is pushed, a v1.3.0 tag would fail at the migrations step (nothing applied).
  - **Database password (owner-checked 2026-10-01):** `supabase migration up` (CLI 2.117) has no `--password` flag (only `--linked`, `--include-all`, `--db-url`); it reads `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD` from the environment, exactly like the old `db push` and the staging script (proven on staging in Preview run 36741998058). Do not add a flag. The `deploy.yml` step calling the script was approved by the owner and pushed 2026-10-01 (cherry-pick of `d9e50fb`).
- **Release plan (updated 2026-10-01):** v1.2.0 shipped phase 4 + the Crew label without 5A (`dd087cc`), v1.2.1 a hotfix with no migration. **v1.3.0 = 5A** (push, email, the bell), tagged on `main` after phase 5A merges and the owner has pushed the `deploy.yml` change above; production then applies 5A's nine migrations through `scripts/production-migrations.sh`. Before tagging: `DRY_RUN_LIST` against a listing is optional; the job's own listing prints the decision. (Superseded: phase 4 gets the tag `phase-4-done` only and **no release of its own**. **`v1.2.0` = phase 4 + 5A (the notification core) + the "Crew" label, released together** once all three are on `main`: production (which still holds only `v1.1.0`'s migrations) then applies phase 4's and 5A's migrations **in date order in one plain `supabase db push`** (`20260930050132` → `20260930070616` → `20260930073623` → phase 5's reconciling migration), so no migration lands out of order and `deploy.yml`'s production step needs no `--include-all`. The go-live runbook's pending staff invites and guide rebuild wait for that release (`docs/runbooks/go-live.md`, owner decision 2026-09-30). **Never tag a release from `main` while it holds phase 4 without 5A.**)
- **Staging migrations with two open phase branches (2026-09-30, owner request during the phase 4 finish; `scripts/staging-migrations.sh`, used by `deploy.yml`'s staging job and `preview.yml`'s `staging migrations` job):** the Preview run on `phase-4` `4c3b8b1` failed at "staging migrations" ("Remote migration versions not found in local migrations directory: 20260930050132 20260930073623", `phase-5`'s 5A migrations already on staging), so `20260930070616_phase4_review_fixes` never reached staging. The script now applies whatever staging is missing even while staging holds another branch's versions: empty stand-ins for those versions in a scratch copy of `supabase/`, then `supabase migration up --include-all` (the CLI applies and records the missing files in version order), then a second listing; never `migration repair`, never an edit of an applied migration. Proved locally on a copy of staging's state (phase-4 through `task_reads` + phase-5's two): reproduced the CLI error, then the script applied and recorded only `20260930070616`, a second run skipped, the dry run agreed. **Consequence on staging, now:** `phase4_review_fixes` and phase-5's `notifications_core` both re-create `task_create`, `task_update_assignment`, `task_submit_done`, `task_review` and `task_set_approver`; staging now runs phase-4's versions (without phase-5's `app.notify` calls), so **the phase-5 preview sends no notifications from those five until phase 5 adds a migration of its own** (the job warns naming both files). **Phase 5 must, when it merges `main` (after phase 4):** its `20260930050132` sorts before phase-4's `20260930070616`, so (a) on a fresh database (CI, local) `070616` runs after `050132` and drops the notification calls from those five functions: phase 5 needs a new migration, sorting after both, that re-creates them with both the phase-4 review fixes (decisions 33–36) and the notifications; (b) **production: settled by the release plan (owner, 2026-09-30)**: phase 4 is not released alone; `v1.2.0` carries phase 4 + 5A + the "Crew" label, so production applies `050132`, `070616`, `073623` and the reconciling migration in date order in one plain `db push` (a release of phase 4 alone would have made `050132` older than production's newest, which a plain push refuses). Nothing in production was touched here.
- **Task page rework mechanics (Kickoff 4 decisions 26–32; read before touching the task page, its views, Chat, `task_reads` or the task specs):** (1) **The views are client state in one page**, not routes: every panel is rendered with the page and shown by CSS on `data-view` (`PANEL` in `task-view.tsx`), so the first paint is right before hydration and a switch costs no request; the URL follows through `history.replaceState` **after `closeOverlaysThen`** (a closed sheet leaves a spent overlay entry; writing the URL on it lost the view on the next back, the 3.4 lesson). Next keys the page without its search params, so a replace never remounts it; `useState` holds the view, so a refresh, an action's answer or a revalidation never switches it (decision 32). (2) **Details is one element** placed by CSS: on a phone the main column is `max-md:contents` so the title bar and the views' bar stick for the whole page (a sticky element inside a wrapper would only stick inside the wrapper), and Details follows the panels; from `md` up it is the sticky right column. (3) **Chat's thread is drawn in one place at a time:** inline until `useIsDesktop()` says phone, then only inside the sheet; the draft lives in `TaskChat`, outside the sheet and the lazy composer, so a back keeps it. A phone arriving on `?tab=chat` opens the sheet once (render-time state, not an effect: `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`). The sheet's keyboard placement is inline style (`bottom` = the keyboard's inset, `height` = the visible height minus 0.75rem), because the Sheet's own `data-[side=bottom]:bottom-0` outranks a plain utility. (4) **Marking read** runs only when something is unread, up to the newest comment's own time (never `now()`, so a comment written meanwhile stays new), in the background (no Retry, a failed mark leaves the count), and revalidates `/tasks` (layout) and `/approvals` so a back to a list drops the marker. (5) **The step dialogs live once, in `TaskNextStep`,** opened from the bar or from ⋯ through the provider's `dialog`; the Done sheet mounts on its first open (render-time `setDoneLoaded`) and stays mounted. (6) **`data-view-tab`, not `data-tab`,** on the view buttons: `data-tab` is the bottom bar's pre-hydration attribute. The views' bar sets `data-live` when it hydrates (`markLive`): **a spec waits for it before tapping a view** (a tap before hydration is lost: found in `tasks.spec` at 375px). (7) **Specs:** `getByLabel("Comment")` also matches the "Comments" list; use `getByRole("textbox", { name: "Comment" })`. Read a task id from `new URL(page.url()).pathname` (the address may carry `?tab=`). The keyboard is stood in by an own `height` property on `window.visualViewport` plus a `resize` event (`openKeyboard` / `closeKeyboard` in `task-page.spec`). A sheet is measured after `animationsSettled`. The held task-page fixtures use a short title (`shortTag`): the title bar is traced and a long title wraps on a phone; the title skeleton sits in the h1's line box (24px, 32px from `md`). `task-page.spec` uses `tasks.spec`'s people on days 31–40 out (never a day `tasks.spec`'s warnings count). (8) **Screens for the phone walk (installed, 375 and 430, both themes, 130% / 200% text):** the task page for the Owner, an approving Admin, a Staff assignee and a coordinator: the title bar with ⋯; the glance line (state dot, flag and priority, relative deadline, red when overdue) and the needed line; the next-step bar (Task Noted, Start work, Mark done / again, "for Asha", Approve and Request changes) and it stepping aside with the keyboard; the views' bar sticky under the title bar and scrolling at 200%; **Work** (brief, stages with Add stage and Remove stage, the hand-in first once handed in, its links, the late reason), **Chat** (the full-height sheet, the thread on its newest comment, your own on the right, "Writing as" for a coordinator, the composer above the keyboard, Send, back closes it, "Chat · 2 new", arriving on `?tab=chat`), **Activity** (five rows, Show all, a collapsed tick line), **Details** (people with Noted, client, type, approval route, created by, fields); ⋯ (Mark done, Decide it yourself, Edit, Change approver, Reopen, Cancel) and each dialog; the loading screen of each view (`?tab=activity`, `?tab=details`, `?tab=chat`); the desktop's two columns; the unread bubble on the Tasks tab rows (each role), All tasks (cards, table) and Approvals' task rows; full names in the Attendance, Leave, Extra work and Expenses approval rows and dialogs, comp leave's dialogs and the /me welcome. View controls added: the task page's views; Show all.
- **4C mechanics (read before touching the task lists, Approvals' task group, the badges, People's freelancer screens, Settings → Task types or Templates, Suggested tasks or the 4C specs):** (1) **The directory is cheap for every role since `894a098`** (`app.directory_visible_ids()`, decision 21): the lists read `member_directory` whole (`src/app/(app)/tasks/reads.ts`, one `cache()`d read per request, shared with the dialog's setup); 4B mechanics (1)'s "read Staff's by ids" is history. (2) **The badges stream** (`NavCount`, ARCHITECTURE §19): the `(app)` layout starts `task_counts()` and the Owner's attendance counts and never awaits them; awaiting them made fast pages lose the race with their loading screen. (3) **The task dialog has three modes** (`TaskFormMode`: `create`, `convert`, `edit`). `convert` starts from a `ConvertedRequest` (title, details as the description, the label and, for the Owner only, `approverId`: the label's Admin, computed on the server from `readLabelClients()`, the same cached read the setup uses) and saves through `convertRequest` → `task_request_convert` (task_create's arguments after `request_id`). **The draft carries the template** (`TaskDraft.templateId`, "" for none): the form itself remounts on "Discard?" → Keep editing, so anything that must survive a back lives in the draft, outside the `Dialog`. An approver the setup no longer offers (a client's Admin who left) is treated as nobody (`approverId` in `TaskForm`). (4) **"Discard?" on a dialog form** is one pattern (the task dialog, the Suggest dialog, the template dialog): the draft's state sits in the component that renders the `Dialog`, `phase: "form" | "discard"`, the `Dialog` open only in `form`, a `ConfirmDialog` ("Discard …" / Keep editing) in `discard`; Cancel and back both go through `requestClose`; a save closes directly. The task types dialog (a name and a kind) has none (Ideas). (5) **Dialogs load on first open** (`next/dynamic`, `ssr: false`): `TaskFormDialog`, `SuggestTaskDialog`, `TemplateDialog`; with the template dialog in its first load `/settings/templates` was 843.1 KB (now 791.7). `/today` and `/my-day` stand at 783.5 of 785 KB: nothing new may reach the shell. (6) **Templates are a plain edit** (RLS + `app.task_templates_guard()`, audited): the service role cannot insert one for someone else (the guard wants `created_by = auth.uid()` outside a transition), so a spec makes a template through the screen or as that person; `removeTemplatesNamed` nulls `tasks.template_id` first. A template's field defaults go through `validateCustomFieldsFor(…, { skipRequired: true })`. (7) **Requests:** RLS decides who sees a request and so who may decide it (`app.task_request_visible`), **except their own** (Kickoff 4 decision 23: `task_request_convert` / `_decline` refuse an Admin's own; `requestActions` offers them Withdraw only); `countRequestsToDecide(viewerId)` counts the pending ones the viewer sees and did not make. "Open the task" follows the task's RLS (`listTaskRequests` embeds `task:tasks!task_id(id)`, null when the viewer may not see it; `opensTask`). An Admin suggests with their own clients only (`readLabelClients()` feeds their Suggest dialog; `task_request_create` refuses another Admin's client, 4C review S8a). (8) **The 4C specs' people and fixtures** (all per project, so the projects run side by side, each file serial): `task-lists.spec` `list-{staff,coord,admin}-<project>` and the freelancers `40000000-…-4..6`; `freelancers.spec` `people-coord[2]-<project>` (the Owner's storage state; a deactivated coordinator is put back in `finally`); `task-settings.spec` flows on the desktop project only (the type order is one shared list); `task-requests.spec` `req-staff-<project>`, `list-admin-<project>`, `task-admin-<project>` as the other Admin (read only), `list-staff-<project>` as another suggester, the client fixture `Req client <project>` (run by `list-admin`). Helpers: `removeRequestsTitled`, `removeTemplatesNamed`, `removeTaskTypesNamed`, `removeFreelancersNamed`. **Gotchas:** `getByLabel("Stage 1")` also matches "Remove stage 1" (use `exact: true`); People shows ten cards at a time on a phone, so a spec opens a person by address; **a loading screen is held by its data, never its document** (4C review M1, `e2e/loading-screens.spec.ts`, which replaced `mobile.spec`'s `LOADING_SCREENS` and `heldShell`): the e2e build and server reach Supabase through `e2e/hold-proxy.ts` (Playwright's first `webServer`, `127.0.0.1:54331`; CI's build step points `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` at it); a test signs its page in with a session of its own (`ownSession`: GoTrue's password grant written into the `sb-127-auth-token` cookie as `@supabase/ssr` writes it) and holds that session's reads of one PostgREST path only the **page** reads (`holdReads(path, session)`; never one the `(app)` layout awaits, or no shell streams), so the page cannot answer before its loading screen and no other test is ever held; the hold is the open connection (release in `finally`; a dying test releases it). Each entry names its marker, its held path and the points its skeleton traces (`{ held, settled }` selectors: the first heading, the first row), checked within 1px after the release, at every width (the desktop project runs it too), plus a per-test fixture when the settled page needs data to show them (a Staff task, a suggestion, a template, a task waiting for the Admin's check). Held paths: Owner `/today` `rpc/attendance_today_detail`, Admin `/today` `extra_work_notes` (the strip's End day reads the overtime note: the seeded Admin's day must be started and not ended, asserted first), `/me` `coordinated_freelancers`, the task screens and an Admin's Approvals `tasks`, Suggested tasks `task_requests`, Task types `task_types`, Templates `task_templates`. **A skeleton passes the page's `description` and `help`** (4C review S7; the copy in one module both import: `settings/headers.ts`, a route's `copy.ts`, `standInDescription`) and sits each bar in its text's line box (`flex h-5 items-center` for a `text-sm` line). **The hold proxy passes WebSocket upgrades and streamed answers untouched** (owner requirement 2026-09-30; a byte-for-byte tunnel, never held; proved by `tests/hold-proxy.test.ts` in `pnpm check`): **phase 5 (5A) must add an e2e check that a Realtime subscription connects through the proxy once the bell lands, and must start Realtime in CI's e2e job (remove `realtime` from its `pnpm supabase start -x …` list).** **Never set `data-slot` on a primitive whose slot the CSS keys on** (`select-trigger`: 44px and 16px; `*dialog-content`, `sheet-content`: overscroll containment): the checkpoint's "Start from" trigger measured 32px. (9) **Screens for the phone walk (4C; installed, 375 and 430, both themes, 130% / 200% text):** **Tasks** for the Owner and an Admin ("Needs you" rows with their reason lines, "N tasks waiting for your approval" into Approvals, "N suggested tasks to decide" into Suggested tasks, Open tasks, "See all …", "Suggested tasks"; New task) and for Staff ("My tasks": Not noted, Changes requested, Due today, Upcoming, Overdue, "For Asha" rows, "N tasks with the reviewers", All my tasks, Your suggestions, the Suggest a task FAB), each skeleton;
